from pathlib import Path
code=r'''"""Exact elementary certificate (3,3) belongs to D.
No unproved IA presentation is used. See companion proof text for ping-pong.
"""
from itertools import product

def red(w):
    a=[]
    for x in w:
        if a and a[-1]==-x:a.pop()
        else:a.append(x)
    return tuple(a)
def inv(w):return tuple(-x for x in w[::-1])
def cat(*ws):return red(x for w in ws for x in w)
def comm(u,v):return cat(u,v,inv(u),inv(v))
unit=((1,),(2,),(3,))
# LEFT Magnus generators, C-first.
names=[('C',1,2),('C',1,3),('C',2,1),('C',2,3),('C',3,1),('C',3,2),('M',1,2,3),('M',2,1,3),('M',3,1,2)]
def gen(g,s=1):
    A=list(unit);typ,i,*leaf=g
    if typ=='C':j=leaf[0];A[i-1]=(s*j,i,-s*j)
    else:
        j,k=leaf;c=comm((j,),(k,));A[i-1]=cat(c if s==1 else inv(c),(i,))
    return tuple(A)
def compose(A,B):
    Ai=[inv(w) for w in A]
    return tuple(cat(*(A[x-1] if x>0 else Ai[-x-1] for x in w)) for w in B)
def evaluate(w):
    A=unit
    for x in w:A=compose(A,gen(names[abs(x)-1],1 if x>0 else -1))
    return A

def mul(a,b,N):
    c={}
    for u,x in a.items():
        for v,y in b.items():
            if len(u)+len(v)<=N:c[u+v]=c.get(u+v,0)+x*y
    return {u:c for u,c in c.items() if c}
def magnus(w,N):
    a={():1}
    for x in w:
        i=abs(x)-1;b={():1,(i,):1} if x>0 else {tuple([i]*k):(-1)**k for k in range(N+1)}
        a=mul(a,b,N)
    return a

def mm(A,B,p=None):
    n=len(A);C=tuple(tuple(sum(A[i][k]*B[k][j] for k in range(n)) for j in range(n)) for i in range(n))
    return tuple(tuple(x%p for x in r) for r in C) if p else C
def eye(n):return tuple(tuple(int(i==j) for j in range(n)) for i in range(n))
def inverse2(A):
    a,b=A[0];c,d=A[1];det=a*d-b*c;assert abs(det)==1
    return ((d//det,-b//det),(-c//det,a//det))
def fox(w,t=(1,-1,1)):
    out=[0,0,0];pre=1
    for x in w:
        i=abs(x)-1
        if x>0:out[i]+=pre;pre*=t[i]
        else:pre*=t[i];out[i]-=pre
    return out

def psi(A):
    J=[fox(w) for w in A]
    # Fundamental Fox identity implies span(row e1,row e3) invariant.
    assert J[0][1]==J[2][1]==0
    return inverse2(tuple(tuple(J[i][j] for j in (0,2)) for i in (0,2)))

def matword(w,images,p=None):
    n=len(images[0]);A=eye(n)
    for x in w:
        B=images[abs(x)-1]
        if x<0:
            if p==2:
                # UT4 exponent divides4 or8; inverse finite geometric series.
                N=tuple(tuple((B[i][j]-int(i==j))%2 for j in range(n)) for i in range(n));P=eye(n);C=eye(n)
                for k in range(1,n):
                    P=mm(P,N,2);C=tuple(tuple((C[i][j]+P[i][j])%2 for j in range(n)) for i in range(n))
                B=C
            else:B=inverse2(B)
        A=mm(A,B,p)
    return A

def transvection(i,j):
    A=[list(r) for r in eye(4)];A[i][j]=1;return tuple(map(tuple,A))

if __name__=='__main__':
    W=comm(comm((9,),(7,)),(1,))
    A=evaluate(W)
    assert A!=unit
    for i,w in enumerate(A,1):assert magnus(cat(w,(-i,)),4)=={():1}
    P=[psi(gen(g)) for g in names]
    D=((-1,0),(0,1));U=((1,2),(0,1));V=((1,0),(-2,1));negD=((1,0),(0,-1))
    assert P==[D,eye(2),eye(2),eye(2),eye(2),negD,U,eye(2),V]
    assert psi(A)==matword(W,P)
    a=transvection(0,1);b=transvection(1,2);c=transvection(2,3)
    R=[a,eye(4),eye(4),eye(4),eye(4),a,mm(b,a,2),eye(4),mm(c,a,2)]
    result=matword(W,R,2)
    assert result==transvection(0,3)
    print('W=',W)
    print('Exact Aut image lengths',[len(w) for w in A])
    print('W acts trivially modulo gamma5(F3): VERIFIED by degree4 Magnus expansion')
    print('Fox homomorphism generator matrices:',P)
    print('Class3 UT4(F2) image of W:',result)
    print('CERTIFIED (3,3) in D; target gamma4 is trivial, W image is nontrivial')
'''
Path('workspace/agent2_D33_certificate.py').write_text(code)
exec(compile(code,'workspace/agent2_D33_certificate.py','exec'))

from pathlib import Path
p=r'''# Unconditional elementary certificate: (3,3) belongs to D

Use the LEFT Magnus automorphisms K_ij:xi->xj xi xj^{-1} and M_i;jk:xi->[xj,xk]xi. Put A=M_3;12, B=M_1;23, C=K_12 and W=[[A,B],C]. Then W belongs to gamma3 IA3.

## W lies in A4

Write X=X1,Y=X2,Z=X3. Degree1 Johnson derivations are
 A(Z)=[X,Y], B(X)=[Y,Z], C(X)=[Y,X], zero on the other letters.
Their bracket E=[A,B] is
 E(X)=-ad(Y)^2 X, E(Y)=0, E(Z)=ad(Y)^2 Z.
It commutes with C, as checked on X,Y,Z. Thus tau3(W)=[[A,B],C]=0. Since gamma3 IA3 is contained in A3, the kernel property of tau3 yields W in A4. The companion checker additionally computes the actual free-group automorphism W and verifies every W(xi)xi^-1 has Magnus expansion 1 through tensor degree4.

## An actual group quotient detects W modulo gamma4

Use row Fox Jacobians abelianized at the character t=(1,-1,1). Every IA automorphism fixes this character. The fundamental Fox identity says J(phi)*(0,-2,0)^t=(0,-2,0)^t, so the row cycle module span(e1,e3) is invariant. The Fox chain rule in right-to-left automorphism composition is J(phi psi)=J(psi)J(phi). Therefore psi(phi)=(J(phi)|cycles)^-1 is a genuine homomorphism IA3->GL2(Z).

Direct Fox differentiation gives the generator images:
 K12 -> D=diag(-1,1), K32 -> -D;
 M1;23 -> U=[[1,2],[0,1]], M3;12 -> V=[[1,0],[-2,1]];
 all other standard Magnus generators -> identity.

Let H0=<D,U,V>. The pair U,V freely generates a free group: on the real projective line, put P={|x|>1}, Q={|x|<1}. Every nonzero power U^m sends Q into P, and every nonzero power V^m sends P into Q, because |x+2m|>1 for |x|<1 and |x/(1-2m x)|<1 for |x|>1. Ping-pong proves freeness, including faithfulness in PSL2, so -I is not in <U,V>. Also D^2=1 and DUD=U^-1, DVD=V^-1. Determinants distinguish D<U,V> from <U,V>, so H0 is exactly F(U,V) semidirect C2 with inversion on both free generators. On putting a=D,b=UD,c=VD, its presentation is <a,b,c | a^2=b^2=c^2=1>, the free product of three order-two groups.

The entire Fox image is H0 times the central group <-I>: K32=-D, while no -I belongs to H0 (determinants rule out its D-coset and ping-pong rules out its free subgroup). Consequently projection killing -I gives an actual homomorphism IA3->H0.

Now map this free product into UT4(F2) by
 a -> I+E12, b -> I+E23, c -> I+E34.
All three are involutions, so the map exists. It takes U=ba and V=ca. Exact matrix multiplication gives
 [[V,U],D] -> I+E14 != I.
The group UT4(F2) has nilpotency class at most3 (strictly upper triangular ideal has fourth power zero), hence gamma4 of this target is trivial. Therefore W is NOT in gamma4 IA3.

Together W in gamma3 IA3 intersection A4 but W notin gamma4 IA3 proves (3,3) in D. No conjectural IA presentation is used anywhere.

Every finite computation above is executed by `workspace/agent2_D33_certificate.py`, which uses only the Python standard library and runs in0.1sec.
'''
Path('workspace/agent2_D33_proof.md').write_text(p)

sys.path.insert(0,'workspace')
from agent2_source_relations import generators
from agent2_cubic_source import IntegralLattice
from agent2_source_defect import insert as insertfield,reduce as reducefield

G=generators(4);N=len(G);P=list(combinations(range(N),2));H=pickle.load(open('workspace/agent2_ia4_GL_relations.pkl','rb'));start=time.time();L=IntegralLattice()
for col in range(H.cols):
    R={}
    for r,(i,j) in enumerate(P):
        c=int(H[r,col])
        if c:R[i,j]=c;R[j,i]=-c
    for k in range(N):
        v={}
        for (i,j),c in R.items():
            a=(i*N+j)*N+k;b=(k*N+i)*N+j
            v[a]=v.get(a,0)+c;v[b]=v.get(b,0)-c
        L.insert(v)
print('n4quadraticconsequence rank',len(L.b),'secs',time.time()-start,flush=True)
gi={g:i for i,g in enumerate(G)};a=gi['M',2,0,1];b=gi['M',0,1,2];c=gi['C',0,1]
W={}
for (i,j,k),v in [((a,b,c),1),((b,a,c),-1),((c,a,b),-1),((c,b,a),1)]:W[(i*N+j)*N+k]=v
v=W.copy()
while v:
    p=min(v);a=v[p]
    if p not in L.b or a%L.b[p][p]:break
    q=a//L.b[p][p]
    for j,c in L.b[p].items():
        b=v.get(j,0)-q*c
        if b:v[j]=b
        else:v.pop(j,None)

for p in [2,3,1000003]:

    res=reducefield(B,W,p)
    print('mod',p,'Qconsequence rank',len(B),'Wremainderterms',len(res),flush=True)
pickle.dump(L.b,open('workspace/agent2_rank4_quadratic_cubic.pkl','wb'))
'''

from pathlib import Path
code=r'''import sys,json,pickle,re,time
sys.path.insert(0,'workspace')
from agent2_source_relations import generators
from agent2_cubic_source import magnus3
from itertools import product
D=json.load(open('workspace/agent4_gap_ia4_data.json'));N=24;off=N*N;start=time.time()
names=[(g[0],)+tuple(map(int,re.findall(r'\d+',g))) for g in D['names']];index={g:i for i,g in enumerate(names)};oldG=generators(4);reindex=[]
for g in oldG:reindex.append(index[(g[0],)+tuple(x+1 for x in g[1:])])
class F2Lattice:
    def __init__(self):self.b={};self.queue=[]
    def insert(self,v):
        v=set(v)
        while v:
            p=min(v)
            if p in self.b:v.symmetric_difference_update(self.b[p])
            else:self.b[p]=v;self.queue.append(v);return True
        return False
    def reduce(self,v):
        v=set(v)
        for p,w in sorted(self.b.items()):
            if p in v:v.symmetric_difference_update(w)
        return v
L=F2Lattice();Q=pickle.load(open('workspace/agent2_rank4_quadratic_cubic.pkl','rb'))
for row in Q.values():
    v=set()
    for idx,c in row.items():
        if c%2:
            i,j,k=idx//(N*N),(idx//N)%N,idx%N;v.add(off+(reindex[i]*N+reindex[j])*N+reindex[k])
    L.insert(v)
print('Initialized Q3 mod2rank',len(L.b),'secs',time.time()-start,flush=True);L.queue=[]
def vector(p):
    v=set()
    for u,c in p.items():
        if not c%2:continue
        if len(u)==2:v.add(u[0]*N+u[1])
        if len(u)==3:v.add(off+(u[0]*N+u[1])*N+u[2])
    return v
for r in D['rels']:L.insert(vector(magnus3(tuple(r[::-1]),N)))
print('Added806seeds totalrank',len(L.b),'newqueue',len(L.queue),'secs',time.time()-start,flush=True)
operators=[]
for e in D['endomorphisms']:
    qs=[magnus3(tuple(w[::-1]),N) for w in e]
    lin=[{u[0] for u,c in q.items() if len(u)==1 and c%2} for q in qs]
    quad=[{u for u,c in q.items() if len(u)==2 and c%2} for q in qs]
    operators.append((lin,quad,{}))
def monomial(op,idx):
    lin,quad,cache=op
    if idx in cache:return cache[idx]
    v=set()
    def toggle(x):
        if x in v:v.remove(x)
        else:v.add(x)
    if idx<off:
        i,j=divmod(idx,N)
        for a,b in product(lin[i],lin[j]):toggle(a*N+b)
        for (a,b),c in product(quad[i],lin[j]):toggle(off+(a*N+b)*N+c)
        for a,(b,c) in product(lin[i],quad[j]):toggle(off+(a*N+b)*N+c)
    else:
        w=idx-off;i,j,k=w//off,w//N%N,w%N
        for a,b,c in product(lin[i],lin[j],lin[k]):toggle(off+(a*N+b)*N+c)
    cache[idx]=v;return v
idx=0
while idx<len(L.queue):
    v=L.queue[idx];idx+=1
    for op in operators:
        w=set()
        for i in v:w.symmetric_difference_update(monomial(op,i))
        L.insert(w)
    if idx%50==0:print('processed',idx,'queue',len(L.queue),'rank',len(L.b),'secs',time.time()-start,flush=True)
C={p-off:{i-off for i in v} for p,v in L.b.items() if p>=off}
a=index['M',3,1,2];b=index['M',1,2,3];c=index['C',1,2]
W={off+(a*N+b)*N+c,off+(b*N+a)*N+c,off+(c*N+a)*N+b,off+(c*N+b)*N+a}
res=L.reduce(W)
print('FINALrank4mixedmod2rank',len(L.b),'purecubicrank',len(C),'oldWremainder',len(res),'time',time.time()-start,flush=True)
pickle.dump(C,open('workspace/agent2_rank4_cubic_F2_source.pkl','wb'))
'''
Path('workspace/agent2_rank4_F2_optimized.py').write_text(code)
exec(compile(code,'workspace/agent2_rank4_F2_optimized.py','exec'))

from pathlib import Path
text=r'''# Arithmetic proof of universal nonvanishing, all n>=3 and c>=3

This document states precisely the classical number-theoretic inputs and proves the reduction/application. It does NOT assume any presentation for IA_n, and it does not use an unjustified rank stabilization map.

## Classical inputs

(1) For a field F and d>=3, the Steinberg group St_d(F) maps onto SL_d(F) with central kernel K_2(F), identified with Milnor symbols {a,b} (Matsumoto). If F is a nonarchimedean local field containing mu_p, the p-th Hilbert symbol gives K_2(F)->mu_p. Pushing out gives the p-fold metaplectic cover of SL_d(F). Root unipotents have canonical lifts; for roots alpha,beta, commuting torus lifts satisfy
 [h_alpha(a),h_beta(b)] = (a,b)_p^{(alpha,beta)}.
For type A, the coroots e_i-e_k and e_i-e_0 have inner product 1 when i,k,0 are distinct.

(2) Let F/Q_p have ramification e, contain mu_p, and have residue field F_p. Put C=pe/(p-1). The Hilbert symbol satisfies
 (U^a,U^b)_p=1 if a+b>C,
 U^{C+1} subset F^{*p},
and, if p does not divide a, its boundary pairing
 U^a/U^{a+1} x U^{C-a}/U^{C-a+1} -> mu_p
is perfect. We use only a=1. Since residue degree is one, any two units with nonzero respective leading coefficients pair nontrivially.

(3) For a number field K, integral K_2(O_K) is unramified (all tame symbols vanish). Away from p the p-th Hilbert symbol factors through the tame symbol. The global Hilbert reciprocity product is 1. Consequently, if K is totally imaginary and has a UNIQUE prime v over p, then
 K_2(O_K) -> K_2(K_v) -> mu_p
is zero.

These are standard Matsumoto/local-symbol/localization/reciprocity theorems, not an Andreadakis theorem.

## Local congruence lemma (proved using the Steinberg relations)

Write Gamma_r={g in SL_d(O_F): g=I mod pi^r}. Let C=pe/(p-1) be integral. In the metaplectic cover choose the Gauss section sigma near I: lift lower unipotent, diagonal, upper unipotent factors in a fixed order.

Claim: if a+b>C then sigma(g)sigma(h)=sigma(gh) for g in Gamma_a,h in Gamma_b.

Indeed, converting the product of their Gauss decompositions to Gauss normal form requires only the elementary root/torus Steinberg relations. The possible central factors are torus symbols with unit arguments in U^a,U^b, or opposite-root symbols (x,1+xy)_p where v(x)>=a,v(y)>=b. The first vanish by (2); for the second, 1+xy belongs to U^{a+b} subset U^{C+1} subset F^{*p}. All other root relations have no central error. The operations preserve the relevant valuations, so induction through Gaussian elimination proves the claim.

It follows that sigma(Gamma_{C+1}) is a subgroup. It is normal in the full preimage E_1 of Gamma_1 and meets the central mu_p trivially. More generally, if a+b>C, then
 [preimage(Gamma_a),preimage(Gamma_b)] subset sigma(Gamma_{a+b}).
To see the commutator statement from the product statement, use gh=[g,h]hg: the extra factor [g,h] has level a+b, and its multiplication with hg has no central error. Central choices of lifts do not affect commutators.

Thus N=E_1/sigma(Gamma_{C+1}) is nilpotent of class at most C. Indeed, ordinary congruence commutators give gamma_C(E_1) subset preimage(Gamma_C), and commuting once more with E_1 lands in sigma(Gamma_{C+1}). The central mu_p embeds in N.

## Global integral lift

Suppose K is as in (3), v is its unique p-prime, F=K_v, and d>=3. The map St_d(O_K)->St_d(F)->metaplectic cover kills ker(St_d(O_K)->E_d(O_K)). For any element of this kernel maps, after stabilization, to integral K_2(O_K), which is killed by (3). Thus there is an ACTUAL GROUP HOMOMORPHISM
 E_d(O_K) -> metaplectic cover of SL_d(F)
lifting the ordinary local matrix homomorphism. No assumed lift of IA or matrix Lie algebra is involved.

## Fields and units for every C=c>=3

If c has an odd prime divisor p, put h=c/p, K0=Q(zeta_p), alpha=zeta_p-1. If c is a power of two, then c>=4: put p=2,h=c/4,K0=Q(i),alpha=i-1.

If h=1, take K=K0, pi=alpha, u=1+pi (a root of unity).
If h>=2, take pi a root of
 P(X)=X^h+alpha X+alpha
and K=K0(pi). This is Eisenstein at the unique prime (alpha) above p. Hence K has a unique p-prime, pi is a local uniformizer, and the residue field is F_p. K is totally imaginary because K0 is. Furthermore
 Norm_{K/K0}(1+pi)=(-1)^h P(-1)=1,
so u=1+pi is a GLOBAL integral unit.

For odd p, the local absolute ramification is e=(p-1)h and pe/(p-1)=ph=c. For p=2, it is e=2h, so 2e=4h=c. Thus the local congruence lemma has C=c in all cases.

## Actual Fox homomorphism in every rank

Fix the three distinct basis indices j=2,i=1,k=3 (additional indices are harmless). Specialize the abelianized Fox Jacobian at t_j=u, all other t_l=1. The fundamental Fox identity implies that the row module spanned by e_l, l!=j, is invariant: J(phi)*(0,...,u-1,...,0)^t equals the same vector, and O_K is a domain. Its restriction is invertible. Since row Fox Jacobians are antihomomorphisms under usual right-to-left composition, take their inverse to obtain a genuine homomorphism
 rho:IA_n -> GL_{n-1}(O_K).

Its Magnus generator images are diagonal u^{-1} on coordinate i for C_ij, and elementary matrices with parameter +/-(u-1) for M_a;jb; all other generators have identity image. In particular its image is generated by these elementary matrices and coordinate unit diagonals.

Embed GL_{n-1} into SL_n by A -> diag(A,det A^{-1}), using an auxiliary coordinate 0. The unit diagonal diag(u^{-1},u) is elementary via the standard root formula h_{i0}(u^{-1}), and all offdiagonal elementary matrices remain elementary. Therefore the Fox homomorphism lands in E_n(O_K), allowing the global integral lift above. It also lands in Gamma_1, so it composes to the class-at-most-c group N constructed above.

## Uniform witness

Use the left Magnus generators
 D=C_12,
 A=M_1;23: x1->[x2,x3]x1,
 B=M_3;12: x3->[x1,x2]x3.
For c>=3 set X=ad_D^{c-3}(A), L=[X,B], W_c=[L,D], with ad_D(X)=[D,X]. Then W_c belongs to gamma_c IA_n.

Its degree-c Johnson derivation is zero. Indeed all three degree-one derivations fix x2 and act linearly on x1,x3 over the polynomial operator t=ad(x2). Their matrices are t times a diagonal matrix and the two opposite matrix units (up to signs). Hence ad_D^{c-3}(A) is +/−t^{c-2} times one matrix unit, L is +/−t^{c-1} times diag(1,-1), and [L,D]=0. Since gamma_c IA_n subset A_c, this gives W_c in A_{c+1}.

Under the Fox homomorphism, D is the unit diagonal u^{-1} on coordinate i, A=x_ik(-pi), B=x_ki(pi). Therefore X is EXACTLY x_ik(r), where
 r=(-pi)(u^{-1}-1)^{c-3}
   =(-1)^{c-2} pi^{c-2} u^{-(c-3)}.
The commutator of x_ik(r),x_ki(pi) has its two-by-two block
 [[1+r*pi+r^2*pi^2, -r^2*pi], [r*pi^2,1-r*pi]].
Put v=1+r*pi+r^2*pi^2. Its Gauss decomposition equals h_ik(v) modulo Gamma_c, because the offdiagonal parameters have valuations at least c. Also
 v=1+epsilon*pi^{c-1} mod pi^c,
where epsilon has residue +1 or -1.

Consequently the image of W_c in N is the central torus commutator
 (v,u^{-1})_p^{(e_i-e_k,e_i-e_0)}=(v,u^{-1})_p.
The discarded root errors lie in Gamma_c, whose commutators with D lie in sigma(Gamma_{c+1}). Since u^{-1}=1-pi mod pi^2, the two leading coefficients are nonzero. The boundary Hilbert pairing of (2), with levels c-1 and1, says this symbol is NONTRIVIAL.

N has class at most c. Thus W_c does NOT belong to gamma_{c+1} IA_n. We have explicitly exhibited
 W_c in gamma_c IA_n intersection A_{c+1} minus gamma_{c+1} IA_n
for every n>=3,c>=3.

Combined with the already established exclusions n=2 or c=1,2, this gives exactly D={(n,c):n>=3,c>=3}.
'''
Path('workspace/agent2_metaplectic_full_proof.md').write_text(text)

```

from itertools import product
class Ring:
 def __init__(self,p,N):self.p=p;self.N=N;self.elts=[self.fromdigits(d) for d in product(range(p),repeat=N)];self.zero=(0,0);self.one=(1,0)
 def fromdigits(self,d):
  a=b=0
  for x in d[::-1]:a,b=-self.p*b,a-self.p*b;a+=x
  return (a,b)
 def norm(self,a,b):
  ds=[]
  for k in range(self.N):
   r=a%self.p;ds.append(r);a-=r;a,b=b-a,-a//self.p
  return self.fromdigits(ds)
 def add(self,x,y):return self.norm(x[0]+y[0],x[1]+y[1])
 def neg(self,x):return self.norm(-x[0],-x[1])
 def mul(self,x,y):
  a,b=x;c,d=y;return self.norm(a*c-self.p*b*d,a*d+b*c-self.p*b*d)
 def scale(self,x,n):return self.norm(n*x[0],n*x[1])
 def power(self,x,n):
  r=self.one
  for k in range(n):r=self.mul(r,x)
  return r
for p,N in [(3,3),(2,4)]:
 R=Ring(p,N);pi=R.norm(0,1);zeta=R.add(R.one,pi);norms=set()
 if p==3:
  for a,b,c in product(R.elts,repeat=3):
   nr=R.add(R.add(R.power(a,3),R.mul(zeta,R.power(b,3))),R.mul(R.power(zeta,2),R.power(c,3)))
   nr=R.add(nr,R.neg(R.scale(R.mul(zeta,R.mul(a,R.mul(b,c))),3)))
   norms.add(nr)
 else:
  for a,b in product(R.elts,repeat=2):norms.add(R.add(R.power(a,2),R.neg(R.mul(zeta,R.power(b,2)))))
 target=R.add(R.one,R.power(pi,N-1))
 print(p,N,'ring',len(R.elts),'normcount',len(norms),'target',target,'innorms?',target in norms)

```

[user message]
For each integer \(n\ge2\), let \(F_n\) be the free group on \(n\) generators. For any group \(G\), define its lower central series by \(\gamma_1(G)=G\) and \(\gamma_{r+1}(G)=[\gamma_r(G),G]\), where \([H,K]\) is the subgroup generated by the commutators \([h,k]=hkh^{-1}k^{-1}\) with \(h\in H\) and \(k\in K\). For every integer \(r\ge1\), define
\[
\mathcal A_r(n)=\ker\!\left(\operatorname{Aut}(F_n)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Aut}\bigl(F_n/\gamma_{r+1}(F_n)\bigr)\right),
\qquad \operatorname{IA}_n=\mathcal A_1(n).
\]
Determine, by explicit conditions on \(n\) and \(c\), the complete set
\[
\mathcal D=\left\{(n,c)\in\mathbb Z_{\ge2}\times\mathbb Z_{\ge1}:\ 
\gamma_{c+1}(\operatorname{IA}_n)\subsetneq
\gamma_c(\operatorname{IA}_n)\cap\mathcal A_{c+1}(n)
\right\}.
\]

The answer was proved recently, so it is not an open problem: your team's task is to find it and prove it, completely, and to write Python that really computes or checks every step that can be checked. Printing what you believe is not evidence. Arriving at an answer that settles less than the question asks (a partial or conditional answer, "not known") is not acceptable, and neither is conceding: when an approach fails, try another.

Proving it means showing that the answer is right in every case the question covers, and that no other answer is. Split the work, and the pieces of each part, between you on Slack.

Stop only when the team's proof is complete. Before you stop, post on Slack what you proved, and what is left.

## Classical number theory inputs (stated precisely)

Let \(F/\mathbb Q_p\) contain \(\mu_p\), have residue field exactly \(\mathbb F_p\), absolute ramification \(e\), and
\[
C=\frac{pe}{p-1}.
\]

1. **Matsumoto / Steinberg cover.** For \(d\ge3\), \(\mathrm{St}_d(F)\to \mathrm{SL}_d(F)\) has central kernel \(K_2(F)\). Push out by the \(p\)-Hilbert symbol \(K_2(F)\to\mu_p\), obtaining a metaplectic cover. Root unipotents lift canonically. For torus lifts, central commutators are Hilbert symbols raised to Cartan pairings:
   \[
   [\widetilde h_\alpha(a),\widetilde h_\beta(b)]
     =(a,b)_p^{(\alpha,\beta)}.
   \]
   For type A coroots \(e_i-e_k\) and \(e_i-e_0\), with three distinct indices, pairing is \(1\). This avoids any factor 2.

2. **Local Hilbert filtration.** Let \(U^r=1+\mathfrak p^r\). Then
   - \((U^a,U^b)_p=1\) if \(a+b>C\);
   - \(U^{C+1}\subset F^{\times p}\);
   - if \(p\nmid a\), the boundary pairing
     \[
     U^a/U^{a+1}\times U^{C-a}/U^{C-a+1}\to\mu_p
     \]
     is perfect, interpreted in Kummer images. We use only \(a=1\).
   Since residue degree is ONE, nonzero coefficients on each side imply the symbol is nontrivial (no trace-zero issue).

3. **Global integral K2 annihilation.** If a totally imaginary number field \(K\) has a UNIQUE prime \(v\mid p\), then
   \[
   K_2(\mathcal O_K)\to K_2(K_v)\xrightarrow{(\cdot,\cdot)_p}\mu_p
   \]
   is zero. Reason: integral K2 is unramified/tame symbols zero; away from p Hilbert symbol factors through tame symbol; complex places contribute 1; global Hilbert reciprocity forces local p contribution 1.

### Why this gives an ACTUAL group hom, not an assumed Lie lift

For \(d\ge3\), map \(\mathrm{St}_d(\mathcal O_K)\to\mathrm{St}_d(F)\to\widetilde{\mathrm{SL}}_d(F)\). Any kernel element of \(\mathrm{St}_d(\mathcal O_K)\to E_d(\mathcal O_K)\), after stabilization, lies in integral \(K_2(\mathcal O_K)\), hence is annihilated by the Hilbert pushout. So the map descends to an actual hom
\[
E_d(\mathcal O_K)\longrightarrow\widetilde{\mathrm{SL}}_d(F).
\]
No Euclidean-ring hypothesis is needed. Unstable Steinberg kernel maps into stable integral K2; that is enough.

## Local congruence/nilpotent quotient lemma

Let
\[
\Gamma_r=\ker(\mathrm{SL}_d(\mathcal O_F)\to
\mathrm{SL}_d(\mathcal O_F/\pi^r)).
\]
Use the canonical **Gauss section** \(\sigma\): lower unipotent lifts, then fixed-order torus lifts, then upper unipotent lifts.

Crucial directly provable fact:
\[
\sigma(g)\sigma(h)=\sigma(gh)
\quad(g\in\Gamma_a,h\in\Gamma_b,\ a+b>C).
\]
Chevalley collection of cross factors introduces only:
- torus symbols \((U^a,U^b)_p\), zero by orthogonality;
- opposite-root symbols \((x,1+xy)_p\), where \(v(xy)\ge a+b>C\), hence \(1+xy\in F^{\times p}\), also zero.

Thus \(\sigma(\Gamma_{C+1})\) is a subgroup, normal in the full preimage \(E_1\) of \(\Gamma_1\), and disjoint from central \(\mu_p\). Also
\[
[\operatorname{pre}\Gamma_a,\operatorname{pre}\Gamma_b]
\subset \sigma(\Gamma_{a+b})\quad(a+b>C).
\]
One way to derive commutator statement from product statement without worrying whether \(\sigma(g^{-1})=\sigma(g)^{-1}\): use
\(gh=[g,h]hg\), and the deep factor \([g,h]\in\Gamma_{a+b}\) multiplies exactly with \(hg\).

Hence
\[
N=E_1/\sigma(\Gamma_{C+1})
\]
is a finite p-group of class at most \(C\): ordinary matrix congruences put \(\gamma_C(E_1)\) inside preimage \(\Gamma_C\), and one more commutator with \(\Gamma_1\) lies in \(\sigma\Gamma_{C+1}\). Central \(\mu_p\) survives in \(N\).

## Number field/global unit for every c>=3

My two-stage construction (valid and in my proof draft):

- If c has an odd prime divisor p, set \(h=c/p\), \(K_0=\mathbb Q(\zeta_p)\), \(\alpha=\zeta_p-1\).
- If c is a power of 2, then c>=4: set p=2, h=c/4, \(K_0=\mathbb Q(i)\), \(\alpha=i-1\).
- If h=1, use K=K0, pi=alpha, u=1+pi.
- If h>=2, pi is a root of
  \[
  X^h+\alpha X+\alpha.
  \]
  Eisenstein at the unique p-prime; K=K0(pi) totally imaginary, unique p-prime, residue Fp.
  \[
  N_{K/K_0}(1+\pi)=(-1)^hP(-1)=1,
  \]
  so u=1+pi is a GLOBAL integral unit.
- Absolute ramification:
  odd p: \(e=(p-1)h\), so C=ph=c;
  p2: e=2h, so C=2e=4h=c.

Agent_3’s cleaner ONE integer polynomial (Slack #298):
- Let \(\ell=p\) odd, r=c/p; or \(\ell=4,p=2,r=c/4\).
- d=phi(ell), always even.
- Define
  \[
  P_c(X)=(X+1)^d\Phi_\ell\!\left(1-\frac{X^r}{X+1}\right).
  \]
- This is a MONIC integer polynomial of degree e=rd, Eisenstein at p:
  mod p cyclotomic polynomial is (Z−1)^d, and P(0)=p.
- P(-1)=1 (d even), so root pi has u=1+pi norm 1.
- K contains \(\zeta_\ell=1-\pi^r/(\pi+1)\), hence totally imaginary.
- Unique p prime, residue Fp, ramification e, conductor C=c.
- Works r=1 with no special case.
- Samples:
  c6: \(X^4-3X^3+6X+3\);
  c8: \(X^4-2X^3+4X+2\).
- `agent3_arithmetic_fields.py` checks exact coefficients/Eisenstein/norm/resultant/conductor c3..64.

## Generic Fox hom and uniform witness

Fix j=2, i=1, k=3, extra n−3 generators harmless.
Specialize abelianized Fox row Jacobian at \(t_j=u\), all other \(t_l=1\). Fundamental identity shows row module span \(e_l,l\ne j\) invariant, since u−1 is nonzero in domain.

Fox row Jacobian is ANTIhom for usual RTL automorphism composition. Two valid hom conventions:
- my draft uses \(\rho(\phi)=J_\phi|_{\rm cycles}^{-1}\);
- agent_3’s simpler convention uses \(\rho=J_\phi^T|_{\rm cycles}\).

All other Magnus generators kill. Kept generators yield coordinate unit diagonals u^{±1} and \(E_{ab}(\pm(u-1))\).
Embed GL_{n−1}(O) into SL_n(O) by diag(A, det A^{-1}), auxiliary coordinate 0. Unit diagonals are elementary \(h_{i0}(u^{\pm1})\); root elementaries remain elementary. Therefore whole IA image lands in \(E_n(O_K)\), so actual global metaplectic lift exists. Image lies in Gamma1.

### Exact convention simplification by agent_3 (#297)

Use transpose Fox hom. Let
- \(A=M_{1;23}\),
- \(B=M_{3;12}\),
- \(D=C_{32}\), NOT C12.
Then
\[
\rho(A)=x_{31}(\pi),\quad
\rho(B)=x_{13}(-\pi),\quad
\rho(D)=h_{3,0}(u).
\]
Hence
\[
X=\operatorname{ad}_D^{c-3}(A)
\]
maps EXACTLY to \(x_{31}(\pi^{c-2})\).
Let \(L=[X,B]\), \(W_c=[L,D]\).
Source weight c. Johnson degree c is zero: D,A,B fix x2 and act linearly on x1,x3 over t=ad(x2); their matrices are t times diagonal and opposite units; L’s matrix is \(\pm t^{c-1}\operatorname{diag}(1,-1)\), commuting with D.

Fox matrix commutator Gauss top-left:
\[
v=1-\pi^{c-1}+\pi^{2c-2}.
\]
Offdiagonal entries have valuation at least c, so \(L\equiv h_{31}(v)\pmod{\Gamma_c}\).
Commutator with D has central image
\[
(v,u)_p^{(e_3-e_1,e_3-e_0)}=(v,u)_p\ne1.
\]
Root errors lie in Gamma_c and commute into split Gamma_{c+1}. Boundary levels c−1,1, coefficients −1,1 give nonzero symbol.
Target N has class≤c, so \(W_c\notin\gamma_{c+1}IA_n\).
Thus universal nonvanishing.

My draft uses D=C12 and inverse Fox hom, yielding a root parameter \((-1)^{c-2}\pi^{c-2}u^{-(c-3)}\), equally valid but messier. Prefer agent_3 exact convention in final.

## n=2, all c excluded
Classical Nielsen theorem IA2=Inn(F2)≅F2. Center of free nilpotent \(F_2/\gamma_{r+1}\) is gamma_r/gamma_{r+1}. Thus A_r(2)=Inn gamma_r(F2)=gamma_r IA2. Therefore no pair n2 in D.

Center proof: first nonzero lower-central Magnus term P of a putative central element commutes with both free associative variables. Centralizer of Xi in free associative algebra is Z[Xi]; intersect two centralizers gives scalars, impossible for positive-degree P.

Magnus generating set K_ij (partial conjugations) and M_i;jk (commutator transvections) has N=n²(n−1)/2 generators. First Johnson sends them to a signed integral basis of \(H^*\otimes\Lambda^2H\). Thus IA_ab exactly this free group, A2=gamma2. No D at c1.

### Additional precise local norm checker note

Current scratchpad’s final persisted prefix will contain harmless imports `Path` and a comment about copying; the shared file deliberately excludes the `# END-CERTIFICATE` marker and self-copy statement, so it can be run independently without overwriting anything.

The checker’s finite-ring normalization formulas are exact:
\[
\pi^2=-p\pi-p,\qquad
(a+b\pi)(c+d\pi)=(ac-pbd)+(ad+bc-pbd)\pi.
\]
To extract the next \(\pi\)-adic digit, take \(r=a\bmod p\), replace \(a\) by \(a-r\), then divide by \(\pi\):
\[
(a,b)\mapsto (b-a,-a/p).
\]
This gives the quotient ring of size \(p^N\). Exhaustive norm absence modulo \(\pi^N\) implies absence of a local field norm because \(\mathcal O_E=\mathcal O_F[\zeta_{p^2}]\) in the two cyclotomic examples and norm reduction is compatible. For the \(p=3\) example, both choices \(1+\pi^2\) and \(1-\pi^2\) should be nonnorms; current code checks plus, which suffices to confirm nondegenerate boundary behavior. For the actual uniform witness the leading coefficient is minus under agent3’s cleaned convention, and one can add the minus target too if desired.

from pathlib import Path
text=r'''# Finite-ring replacement of the metaplectic construction

This eliminates the Gauss-section/local-congruence lemma and global reciprocity entirely.

## Standard K-theoretic/local inputs, precise scope

(1) For R=Z[T,T^{-1}], stable K2(R)=K2(Z) direct-sum K1(Z)=(Z/2)^2, by the fundamental theorem for regular rings. The stable Steinberg extension St(R)->E(R) has this central kernel. Functoriality holds for all ring homomorphisms.

(2) If O is a complete DVR with residue field of cardinality at least 7, and B=O/pi^c, then unit-symbol Milnor K2 agrees with stable K2 for O and B. The reduction K2(O)->K2(B) is onto and its kernel is generated by {1+pi^c a,b}, a in O, b in O^*. This follows immediately from unit-symbol presentations: every unit lifts, and every Steinberg relation {x,1-x}=1 lifts because x and 1-x are units iff their residues are nonzero.

(3) For a local field F containing mu_p, absolute ramification e, C=pe/(p-1), its p-Hilbert pairing has (U^a,U^b)=1 for a+b>C, and the critical pairing at a+b=C, p not dividing a, is perfect in the Kummer graded pieces. For residue F_p and a=1 both pieces have dimension one, so two nonzero coefficients pair nontrivially. Hilbert symbols commute with unramified restriction: on elements from the base field the new symbol is the old one raised to the extension degree. The square of the fourth Hilbert symbol is the quadratic symbol.

## Local ring for each c>=3

If c has an odd prime divisor p, put ell=p and r=c/p. Otherwise c is a power of two >=4; put p=2, ell=4, r=c/4. Put

  P(X)=Phi_ell(1+X^r).

This is monic Eisenstein at p of degree e=r*phi(ell), since Phi_ell(Z) modulo p is (Z-1)^phi(ell), and P(0)=p. A root pi defines a totally ramified local field F0/Qp with residue Fp, containing zeta_ell=1+pi^r. Its critical conductor C is exactly c. The symbol

  (1-pi^(c-1),1+pi)_p

is nontrivial by the critical pairing: indices c-1 and 1 are prime to p, leading coefficients -1 and 1, residue field Fp.

Take an unramified extension F/F0 of degree f prime to p and with residue cardinality >=7 (f=3 for p2; f=2 for p3 or p5; f=1 for p>=7). Nontriviality persists by restriction. Write O=O_F and B=O/pi^c.

For p odd, let lambda:K2(O)->mu_p be the p-Hilbert symbol. For p2, let lambda be the fourth Hilbert symbol modulo mu2, with values mu4/mu2. In both cases lambda kills ker(K2(O)->K2(B)): every b in O^* is a Teichmuller unit times a principal unit; its prime-to-p-order Teichmuller part is a pth power. For the principal unit use (U^c,U^1)_p=1. In the p2 case lambda is trivial iff its square (the quadratic Hilbert symbol) is trivial. Consequently lambda descends to a character chi:K2(B)->mu_p (or mu4/mu2).

The boundary symbol {1-pi^(c-1),1+pi} has nonzero chi. The image of K2(R), under T->1+pi, is killed by chi. Indeed it factors through K2(O), and K2(R) has exponent two. For p odd this is immediate; for p2 the fourth Hilbert symbol on an order-two element lies in mu2, which disappears in the quotient. This last argument uses the LIFT to K2(O), not a claim that every order-two element of K2(B) is killed.

## Canonical nilpotent detector

Push out St(B)->E(B) by chi to obtain central extension 1->mu->Etilde(B)->E(B)->1. Functoriality and vanishing of chi on K2(R) give an actual homomorphism

  E(R) -> Etilde(B).

For the ideal J=(pi) in B, J^c=0. Therefore the fixed-rank congruence matrix group Gamma(J)=ker(SL_d(B)->SL_d(B/J)) has nilpotence class <=c-1, because [1+M(J^a),1+M(J^b)] subset 1+M(J^(a+b)). Its preimage in Etilde(B) has class <=c. The center mu survives tautologically in the pushout.

## Fox map and witness (all n>=3)

Let j=2, i=1, k=3; set t_j=T, other abelian variables=1. The abelianized Fox row Jacobian is an antihomomorphism on IA_n. Its transpose is a homomorphism preserving the cycle module with basis indexed by l!=j. The fundamental Fox identity proves invariance over the domain R: the j-coordinate of every cycle-column is zero because T-1 is nonzero. Restrict to obtain rho:IA_n->GL_(n-1)(R). Append determinant inverse as an extra coordinate 0. This gives a homomorphism to SL_n(R), and actually to E_n(R): every Magnus generator is either identity, an elementary root x_ab(+(T-1)) or x_ab(-(T-1)), or a diagonal h_a0(T). Every image is identity modulo T-1. Thus the preceding homomorphism yields a homomorphism from IA_n to a group of class <=c.

Use Magnus generators

 A=M_1;23 : x1 -> [x2,x3]x1,
 B=M_3;12 : x3 -> [x1,x2]x3,
 D=C_32 : x3 -> x2*x3*x2^-1.

Their determinant-augmented Fox matrices are

 rho(A)=x_31(s), rho(B)=x_13(-s), rho(D)=h_30(T), s=T-1.

Define X_3=A, X_(c+1)=[D,X_c], and W_c=[[X_c,B],D]. Equivalently X_c=ad_D^(c-3)(A). Then X_c has source weight c-2 and Fox matrix x_31(s^(c-2)) EXACTLY. Hence W_c lies in gamma_c(IA_n).

For its Johnson term, put t=ad(x2) in the free Lie algebra. The three derivations fix x2 and every other coordinate, and act linearly on x1,x3 as

 A:x1->t*x3, B:x3->-t*x1, D:x3->t*x3.

Thus X_c:x1->t^(c-2)*x3. Its bracket with B is the diagonal derivation (x1->t^(c-1)*x1, x3->-t^(c-1)*x3), commuting with D. Therefore tau_c(W_c)=0 and W_c belongs to A_(c+1).

Finally compute in St(B), modulo its central kernel. Put a=pi^(c-2), b=-pi and v=1+ab=1-pi^(c-1). The 2x2 matrix commutator [x_31(a),x_13(b)] is exactly h_31(v) in B: offdiagonal errors a^2b and ab^2 vanish since their valuations are >=c, as does a^2b^2. Thus any Steinberg lift of this commutator differs from h_31(v) only by a central element. Any lift of D differs from h_30(1+pi) only by a central element. Taking their commutator eliminates all such central discrepancies and gives

 W_c -> {v,1+pi}^((e3-e1,e3-e0)) = {v,1+pi},

from pathlib import Path
code=r'''"""Exact universal Fox/current-matrix and finite-ring checks for W_c."""
import sympy as S
from math import prod
x,a,b,u=S.symbols('x a b u')
I=S.eye(2); E=S.Matrix([[0,1],[0,0]]); F=E.T
# Cycle-coordinate order is (3,1), so root alpha=e3-e1 is E12.
A=I+x*E; B=I-x*F; D=S.diag(1+x,1)
comm=lambda P,Q:S.simplify(P*Q*P.inv()*Q.inv())
assert comm(D,A)==I+x*x*E
# Entire group commutator identity, before truncation.
C=comm(I+a*E,I+b*F)
assert C==S.Matrix([[1+a*b+a*a*b*b,-a*a*b],[a*b*b,1-a*b]])
# Current Lie matrices: t is the adjoint action of x2.
t=S.symbols('t'); d=S.diag(t,0)
for c in range(3,129):
    Y=t**(c-2)*E
    assert d*Y-Y*d==t*Y
    L=Y*(-t*F)-(-t*F)*Y
    assert L*d-d*L==S.zeros(2)
    # Modulo pi^c, the two off-diagonals and quadratic diagonal correction vanish.
    assert min(2*c-3,c,2*c-2)>=c
    cc=C.subs({a:x**(c-2),b:-x}).applyfunc(S.expand)
    reduce=lambda z:S.Poly(z,x).rem(S.Poly(x**c,x)).as_expr()
    vv=1-x**(c-1)
    assert cc.applyfunc(reduce)==S.diag(vv,1+x**(c-1))
    # Local ramified fields Phi_ell(1+X^r), p odd divisor or p=2.
    odds=[p for p in S.factorint(c) if p%2]
    p=min(odds) if odds else 2
    ell=p if p%2 else 4
    r=c//(p if p%2 else 4)
    P=S.Poly(S.cyclotomic_poly(ell,x).subs(x,1+x**r),x)
    e=r*int(S.totient(ell))
    assert P.degree()==e and P.LC()==1 and P.TC()==p
    assert all(int(z)%p==0 for z in P.all_coeffs()[1:])
    assert int(P.TC())%(p*p)!=0
    assert e*p//(p-1)==c
    f=3 if p==2 else 2 if p in (3,5) else 1
    assert p**f>=7 and f%p
print('PASS exact commutator identity; current-Lie Johnson kernels, truncated torus matrices, and local-field Eisenstein/conductor constructions for 3<=c<=128')
'''
Path('workspace/agent2_finite_ring_checks.py').write_text(code)
exec(compile(code,'workspace/agent2_finite_ring_checks.py','exec'))
